```markdown
# All mass from null edges

**A machine-verified account of where mass comes from, built from a single
primitive: light-speed transport that disagrees with itself.**

Draft v1, 2026-07-08. Status: **[DRAFT-MS]**. This is the *all-mass*
manuscript; it subsumes and cites the P1 origin-of-mass draft
(`Null_Edge_P1_Origin_of_Mass_Manuscript_Draft_v3.md`) rather than
replacing it.

Every technical claim below carries a grade, and the grades are the
point of the paper as much as the claims are:

| Grade | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **T** | source-verified theorem (external mathematics) |
| **M** | machine-verified: kernel-checked in Lean 4 under the pinned toolchain, axiom-audited, guard-pinned |
| **MEMO** | expert-verified prose; kernel transcription pending |
| **C** | pre-registered conjecture with an explicit kill condition |
| **[import]** | an external result used as input, not reproved here |

The discipline this paper holds itself to: **a sentence that claims more
than its grade licenses is an error, however true it may turn out to be.**
Two long-standing conjectures of this program died this month by their own
pre-registered tests (§10). We report those with the same prominence as
the theorems, because a program that cannot say what it has *disproved*
cannot be trusted about what it has proved.

---

## 1. Thesis and reading guide

One sentence: **mass is the obstruction to coherent null transport.**

Unpacked: the only primitive is a *null edge* — an elementary step that
moves at the speed of light, the way a photon does. Nothing in the theory
is slow, and nothing is heavy, at the bottom. Bind several such steps into
one object and ask whether the bundle can still move at light speed. If
its constituent light-directions all agree, it can, and it is massless.
If they disagree, the bundle as a whole cannot keep up with light, and the
amount by which it falls short *is* its mass. Mass is trapped, mutually
disagreeing light.

The organizing slogan of the formal work is **"unification is
decomposition."** A single finite operator — the carrier Dirac operator —
squares to a sum of four terms, and each term is one physical channel
through which mass enters: aperture (kinetic), closure (gauge / QCD), turn
(Higgs / Yukawa), and soldering-gradient (gravity). We do not unify the
forces by identifying them; we unify them by exhibiting them as four
summands of one square (§4).

**How to read this paper.** Part I (§2) is written for a reader who has
seen special relativity and a little quantum mechanics — no gauge theory
assumed. From §3 onward the grades take over and the register is
technical. A reader who wants only the verified core can read §3 (the one
trusted theorem), §4 (the decomposition that organizes everything), and
the anchor table (§11), and skip the rest.

---

## 2. Part I: what is a particle, and what is mass?

*(This section makes no new claims; it is the physical picture behind the
mathematics, in plain language.)*

**A particle is a knot of trapped light.** Picture an electron not as a
tiny ball but as light caught zig-zagging: a left-moving light-step, then
a right-moving light-step, alternating forever. Each leg races at the
speed of light. But a zig-zag that reverses on itself does not *get*
anywhere fast — its average progress is slow, and a particle "at rest" is
the extreme case where the legs cancel and the light runs in place. This
is the old idea that a massive particle is light that has been trapped, and
this program's central theorem (§3) makes it exact: **the mass of a bundle
of light-steps is precisely the total disagreement among their
directions.** All directions parallel: no disagreement, no mass, and the
thing flies off at light speed like a photon. Any disagreement: mass.

**Where mass is made: the corners.** The mass lives not in the legs of the
zig-zag but in the *corners* — the events where the light changes
direction. Take away the ability to turn, and the particle runs straight
at light speed forever, massless. So "how much mass" and "how often it
turns" are the same question. And a turn is not free: turning a
left-handed mover into a right-handed one changes a bookkeeping quantity
(weak charge) that must balance, and the thing that balances it is a field
filling all of space — the Higgs. In this picture the Higgs is not an
optional extra; it is the entry the corner *requires* in order to exist
(§5).

**What a particle is made of: strands.** Internally, a particle's identity
is a short list — which of a few elementary "strands" it carries. Charge
is the bookkeeping of that list: quarks and leptons differ by how many
color strands they hold, which is why quark charges come in thirds; lepton
number and baryon number are just *counts* of strands (§ and the Q04
analysis). An antiparticle is the same list read backwards. The whole
particle zoo of one generation is the catalogue of ways to occupy a
handful of strands.

**Why particles are stable: topology.** Some patterns cannot come apart,
not because a force holds them but because a *count* forbids it. When the
left-handed and right-handed slots fail to balance, the surplus cannot
find a partner to turn with, and so it cannot acquire mass no matter what
fields you switch on. It stays massless the way a knot stays knotted (§8).
This is the program's reading of why masslessness in the Standard Model
tracks chirality.

**Nothing moves slower than light — fundamentally.** Every edge is null.
The electron on your desk is, in this ontology, moving at light speed the
entire time — it simply is not *going* anywhere, because its light-steps
disagree and cancel. "Slower than light" is what the statistics of
disagreeing light-steps looks like from far away. There is no slow
substance underneath.

That is the entire picture. The rest of the paper is the mathematics that
makes each italicized claim precise, and honest about which are theorems
and which are still hopes.

---

## 3. The kinematic layer: one trusted theorem (**T**/**M**)

Everything orbits a single kernel-checked identity. Represent a massless
degree of freedom by a two-component Weyl spinor `psi`; its contribution
to energy-momentum is the rank-one Hermitian matrix `psi psi^dagger`. A
particle-like system is a finite bundle of these, with total momentum
`P = sum_i psi_i psi_i^dagger`. Then

```text
det P = sum_{i<j} | psi_i wedge psi_j |^2 .
```

The left side is invariant mass squared. The right side is the total
pairwise *disagreement* of the null directions — the sum of squared
wedges, which vanishes exactly when two directions are parallel. So:

- A single null edge is massless: `det (psi psi^dagger) = 0`
  (`det_rankOneHermitian_eq_zero`, **M**, trusted namespace).
- Mass equals total pairwise disagreement for any finite bundle
  (`fin_bundle_plucker_mass_identity`, **M**).
- Mass is exactly zero iff all directions are projectively collinear —
  one common beam (`fin_bundle_mass_zero_iff_common_direction`, **M**).

This is the precise form of "mass is trapped disagreeing light," and it is
the strongest thing the program owns: kernel-checked, axiom-audited, in the
trusted layer. It is also *only* kinematics — it says what mass *is* for a
given bundle, not what dynamics builds the bundle. The rest of the paper is
about the dynamics, and it is held to a lower grade for exactly that
reason.

---

## 4. The organizing spine: the mass-budget decomposition (**M** + **C**)

The dynamical object is the finite carrier Dirac operator on a finite
2-complex,

```text
D = sum_e c(alpha_e) nabla_e + Gamma phi ,
```

with a null covector soldering `c(alpha_e)` on each edge (a Clifford
coefficient, `c(alpha)^2 = 0`), a covariant transport `nabla_e`, and a
vertex "turn" term `Gamma phi`. Its square decomposes, as a kernel-checked
algebraic identity, into four channels:

```text
4 . D^2 = Q_A + Q_C + 4 Q_T
```

(`carrier_square_assembly`, **M**), where `Q_A` is the aperture (kinetic)
block tied to the Plücker mass of §3, `Q_C` the closure block built from
commutators of transports (the gauge / QCD channel, §6), and `Q_T = phi^2`
the turn block (the Higgs / Yukawa channel, §5). The soldering-gradient
block `E` (gravity, §7) appears in the Krein-adjoint version `4 D^#D`,
which is the four-slot form.

**Unification is decomposition.** These are not four theories glued
together; they are four summands of one square. The claim the program
stakes is that *the* invariant — pairwise null disagreement — reappears in
each channel through a different canonical map.

**The budget theorem (M).** Given the decomposition and any linear
expectation `ev` (the state functional `<psi, . psi>`) with `ev(D^2) != 0`,
the channel shares of `M^2 = 4 ev(D^2)`,

```text
b_A + b_C + b_T = 1 ,
```

is kernel-checked (`signed_budget_sum_one`, **M**), with a concrete
non-vacuous witness: a single-edge `2x2` carrier gives closure share
exactly zero (one edge, no closure) and budget `(1/2, 0, 1/2)`
(`witness_budget_sum_one`, **M**). Two honesty rails, both load-bearing:

1. **The shares are signed.** We do *not* call them positive fractions.
   Positivity of a channel share is exactly the open crux of §6/§8, and
   until it closes, `b_C` may be negative on some states. This is not a
   defect: §8 explains why the physics of chiral symmetry breaking
   *requires* the closure channel to have negative directions.
2. **`b_C` is the chromomagnetic share, not "gluon energy."** The closure
   *channel* `Q_C` is linear in field strength (a `sigma·F` /
   chromomagnetic object); the `|F|^2` gluon *energy* density is a
   different object (the Wilson action, §6). Conflating them is a
   pre-registered error (§10).

The physical target this shape is aimed at — a finite analogue of the Ji
decomposition of the proton mass — is grade **C**: the weak claim
(non-turn dominance, `|b_T|` small, matching that ~99% of nucleon mass is
not Higgs-generated `[import]`: Yang et al., proton mass decomposition) is
the honest first goal; the strong claim (closure is the single largest
share) is a separate, harder, falsifiable test.

---

## 5. Turn mass: the Higgs-shaped channel (**M** + a reported kill)

The turn block `Q_T = phi^2` is where mass enters at a corner. The
mechanism, in the program's language: a corner converts a left-handed
light-mover into a right-handed one; the two carry different weak charge;
the corner must therefore exchange weak charge with a background
condensate; that condensate is the Higgs. The corner amplitude *is* the
mass. This is a **MEMO**-grade reading, resting on the kinematic corner
identities (`onshell_wedge_normSq_eq_coin_sq`, **M**) and the Standard-
Model strand bookkeeping (Q04, **MEMO**).

**A reported kill (this is the honest heart of the section).** The program
attempted to derive the *value* of the charged-lepton mass ratios — the
Koide relation `Q = 2/3` — from corner geometry, via a soldering
coefficient `kappa` that would have to equal 1. A pre-registered numerical
probe measured it: `kappa = 3/2`, not 1, predicting `Q = 5/9` against the
observed `2/3`, and the carrier reduction does not even produce the
required uniform-diagonal form. **The tetrahedral-corner Koide route is
dead** (probe P1, `TSOLDER_KAPPA_ANALYSIS.md` §4a). What survives is the
equipartition trace identity behind the Koide *combination* (pure algebra,
unaffected) and a sharper open question — see §8. Any future mass-value
route must additionally clear the Sumino bar `[import]`: a real Koide
mechanism must survive QED running, which this route never reached. The
honest status of mass *values* in this program is therefore: **no live
prediction**; ratios, not absolute scales, are the only admissible targets
(§10).

---

## 6. Closure mass: the QCD-shaped channel (**M** + the central open crux)

Most visible mass is QCD binding energy. In this program it lives in the
closure channel, and the closure channel is where the program is,
surprisingly, furthest along outside pure kinematics.

**The Wilson action is a squared closure defect (M).** Before any carrier
identification, the standard lattice gauge action is *exactly* the squared
norm of the failure of transport to close around a face: for a face
holonomy `U`,

```text
Tr((1 - U)^dag (1 - U)) = 2N - 2 Re Tr U ,
```

so the Wilson plaquette weight `N - Re Tr U` is half the Hilbert-Schmidt
square of the closure defect `1 - U`
(`wilson_plaquette_eq_half_closure_defect`, **M**;
`closure_defect_trace_eq`, **M**). QCD's action and the program's closure
channel are the same object at the source. The static-pair potential then
reads as the transfer-time cost of excess areal closure defect (**C**,
Amendment A2).

**The strong-coupling pillars are kernel-checked (M).** On concrete finite
lattices: the Wilson-loop area law (`tyAreaLaw_slab_exp`), slab reflection
positivity (`wilsonSlabConnected_reflectionPositive`), an OS-reconstructed
spectral gap (`osSpectralGap_pos`), and exponential clustering
(`slab_exponential_clustering`) — the two hard pillars of confinement and
mass gap, in their strong-coupling forms. The one remaining hole in the
gap chain is a finite forest-counting injection, now diagnosed (this
month) as a *malposed statement* rather than a hard proof: the total-block
permutation count collapses under the root-pinning constraint, so the
structured-partition route is the only viable one
(`PolymerKPConclusion.lean`; K1-STEP0 audit).

**The closure channel is an exact Krein square — and this relocates the
crux (M + MEMO).** The nonabelian closure block factors exactly:

```text
Q_C = L^# L ,   L = c(alpha_1) (x) 1 + c(alpha_2) (x) (-K/2),
      K = [nabla_1, nabla_2] ,
```

for any compact gauge group, with the full family of representations a
GL-torsor (`null_soldered_square`, `closure_current_square`, **M** for the
abstract algebra; the concrete two-transport identification is **MEMO**,
oracle-verified across SU(2) and SU(3)). But a Krein square carries no
positivity by itself — null Clifford coefficients are isotropic, so the
square has no positive-definite diagonal. Therefore:

> **The central open crux (C).** Positivity of the closure channel is not
> a full-space fact and never could be. It can hold only on the physical
> (Gauss-law) sector, and — because every torsor representative squares to
> the *same* `Q_C` — the decision is a single finite computation: the
> inertia of `Q_C` restricted to the physical sector `V'/N`. If that
> restricted form is positive, closure positivity holds; if indefinite,
> `Q_C` is honestly a signed chromomagnetic channel and positivity is
> demanded only of the total operator on the quotient. This is gate
> **S1-CC**, blocked only on transcribing `V'`.

The existing finite Kugo–Ojima witnesses bracket the two poles: a
nonvacuous positive physical sector on the unbalanced `(2,1)` model
(`nonvacuous_positive_sector`, **M**) and the matching indefinite no-go
(`nondegenerate_but_indefinite_no_go`, **M**). Scope, stated plainly:
everything here is finite and strong-coupling; the continuum Yang–Mills
mass gap is the Clay problem and is **not claimed** (§10).

---

## 7. Soldering mass: the gravity-shaped channel (**M** + **C**)

The soldering-gradient block `E` is the gravity-shaped channel: it
measures how the null soldering fails to be covariantly constant. What is
kernel-checked is narrow and honest: for constant soldering `E` vanishes
(the flat case). What is conjectured (**C**, Amendment/finding 11) is a
finite "geometric trinity" split,

```text
2 E = Contract(T) + Contract(S) ,
```

with `T` the antisymmetrized soldering difference (torsion-shaped) and `S`
a symmetric remainder (non-metricity-shaped) — provided the `E`-contraction
commutes with the index swap; otherwise a mixed term is unavoidable. The
program's earlier conjecture that `E` is *pure* torsion was killed by a
probe (§10); the trinity split is the corrected target, with the discrete
teleparallel / symmetric-teleparallel literature `[import]` (Pereira–Vargas
and Regge-adjacent work) as the anchor. This is the least-developed
channel; the honest content is mostly the boundary, per the Malament split:
causal order supplies the light-cone structure for free, and the
decorations owe exactly the scale.

---

## 8. Protected masslessness: topology forbids mass (**M**)

Some modes cannot acquire mass, and this is a theorem, not a tuning. The
finite McKean–Singer index family shows that for a rank-symmetric carrier
the chiral index equals the graded dimension
(`chiralIndex_eq_graded_dimension`, **M**), and an unbalanced count forces
an exact massless mode immune to every potential and transport
(`exists_protected_massless_mode`, **M**). Masslessness of the chiral
surplus is topological — the knot of Part I, made precise.

**A new protection mechanism, found this month (M + C).** A determinant-
parity probe redirected a stalled line of work: the protected zero modes of
the decorated transport cycle are *not* forced by cyclic symmetry (that
reading was falsified — abstract symmetric data is generically unpinned),
but by a **chiral** symmetry — an involution `Gamma` with
`Gamma W Gamma = W^dagger`, which is exactly the edge-orientation-reversal
grading that also gives the program's Ginsparg–Wilson structure. Its
kernel-checked core: a unitary carrying such an involution has determinant
`+-1` (`chiral_det_eq_pm_one`, **M**), which pins the parity of the
protected-mode count. The full amplitude-independent double pinning
(observed for every hop strength) is grade **C**, awaiting the chiral
winding invariant `[import]` (Asbóth–Obuse, chiral quantum walks). The
resulting spectrum on the small cycle is neutrino-shaped (one exactly
massless mode) — which is where the mass-value question, having failed for
charged leptons (§5), honestly relocates.

**Why indefiniteness is a feature, not a bug.** The closure channel's
global indefiniteness (§6) is *required* here: chiral symmetry breaking
needs the curvature term to pull eigenvalues toward zero against the
positive kinetic part, so a positive-definite closure channel would have
killed this mechanism outright. §6 and §8 are coupled in the right
direction: the same sign structure that blocks naive closure positivity is
what makes protected and near-protected light modes possible.

---

## 9. Dynamics: mass generation under coarse-graining (**M**)

The kinematic theorem (§3) says what mass *is*; this section's theorem says
coarse-graining *makes* it, from the same invariant. One decimation
(Schur-complement) step on a null chain — integrating out a hidden site —
converts square-zero (null) edge terms into a non-null effective term. The
abstract law is

```text
(a b)^2 = k . (a b)   for   a^2 = b^2 = 0,  a b + b a = k . 1
```

(`null_pair_prod_sq_eq_pairing_smul`, **M**): the effective term fails to
be nilpotent exactly when the pairing `k` is nonzero — and for null
directions, nonzero pairing means non-collinear, i.e. *disagreeing*. On the
concrete three-site chain the induced edge is a nonzero idempotent
(`effective_edge_not_nilpotent`, **M**), where none existed before
blocking. The negative control is what makes this a statement about mass:
collinear couplings produce exactly zero effective coupling
(`collinear_schurComplement_eq_zero`, **M**) — nullity survives blocking
precisely on the massless configurations.

So the program's thesis is two-sided, and both sides are kernel-checked:
*kinematically* mass is pairwise null disagreement (§3); *dynamically*,
coarse-graining converts that same disagreement into an effective mass
term, and converts nothing when there is no disagreement. Claim boundary:
one finite decimation step — no renormalization-group flow, no fixed
point, no continuum. The bridge from this step to constituent-mass
generation (blocking increases the finite near-zero count of §6/S4a) is
grade **C**, gated.

---

## 10. Boundaries, and the things we have disproved

**The permanent boundary.** No continuum limit is claimed. No physical mass
scale (dimensional transmutation) is claimed. Nothing Clay-adjacent is
claimed. The only sanctioned limit language is the refinement-ladder
(quotient-then-limit) discipline, and the only admissible mass targets are
dimensionless ratios protected by finite structure — never absolute MeV
values (finding 9; NuFIT-6.0 `[import]` for the one neutrino ratio that is
a legitimate finite target).

**The kills — reported with the prominence of the theorems.** A reader
familiar with the field will expect several natural ideas; here is why each
is dead, so no one re-derives them:

- **Koide from tetrahedral corner geometry** — killed by measurement
  (`kappa = 3/2`, probe P1). The equipartition identity survives as algebra
  (§5).
- **"`Tr E` = discrete torsion"** — killed by probe; replaced by the
  trinity-split target (§7).
- **"`Q_C` = site-diagonal defect Gram"** — killed structurally (grading:
  `Q_C` is purely off-diagonal, orthogonal to every site-local Gram). The
  defect Gram is a real object — it is the Wilson action — just not this
  operator (§6).
- **"`Q_C` is the positive gluon-energy share"** — killed by the
  chromomagnetic distinction: `Q_C` is linear in `F` (hyperfine-shaped);
  the `|F|^2` energy is the defect Gram (§4, §6).
- **Cyclic symmetry forces the protected zero mode** — falsified; the
  correct mechanism is chiral, not cyclic (§8).
- **Retardedness alone deletes fermion doublers** — killed by a
  determinant-level obstruction; one-sided Ginsparg–Wilson inversion is
  false nonabelian (explicit counterexample); the palindromic transfer
  ordering is the correct convention.
- **Spectral-measure language before positivity** — embargoed
  program-wide; finite eigenvalue-*count* identities are the sanctioned
  form (§6's Banks–Casher count, `banks_casher_count`, **M**).

**The open cruxes, ranked.** (1) The physical-sector positivity inertia
(S1-CC, §6) — one finite computation once `V'` is transcribed. (2) The
strong-coupling gap's forest injection (§6) — now a well-posed
combinatorics problem. (3) The multi-direction closure square (a
stabilization, largely dissolved). (4) The chiral winding invariant for
the double-pinning (§8). Each is finite, each has a kill condition, none
requires new axioms.

---

```lean
/-
# S1 closure-current algebra: Lean rungs L1-L3 and an abstract K2/L4 rung

DRAFT (kernel-clean; no `s o r r y`). Transcribes the first three rungs of
the S1 closure contribution
(`AgentTasks/twoday-carrier-run-2026-07-07/s1-closure/`, adopted as
Amendment B of `Sources/Null_Edge_QCD_Mass_Roadmap_2026-07-07.md`; oracle
`Scripts/oracle/probe_s1_closure_oracle_v01.py`, findings F1/F2/F4):

* **L1 (SU(2) centrality).** For a 2x2 unitary with determinant 1, the
  per-face closure Gram is central: `(1-W)(1-W)^H = (2 - tr W) . 1`.
  Route: the generic 2x2 identity `W + adjugate W = (tr W) . 1`
  (`fin_two_add_adjugate`) plus `W^H = adjugate W` for special-unitary
  `W`. The scalar evaluation `2 - 2 cos t = 4 sin^2(t/2)` is included
  (`two_sub_two_cos`): the per-face closure Gram is the same
  `1 - cos` obstruction as the kernel-pinned two-edge aperture mass.
* **L2 (per-face split).** For any unitary `W`:
  `2 (1 - W) = (1-W)(1-W)^H + (W^H - W)` - the closure defect splits
  exactly into a Gram (energy-shaped, Hermitian) part and a
  field-strength (skew) part. Finding F1; the two parts scale as `a^4
  |F|^2` and `a^2 F` respectively (oracle R1.4), which is the
  chromomagnetic-vs-energy wording rail of Amendment B.
* **L3 (null-soldered square, abstract).** In any ring with an
  anti-homomorphism `#` fixing the two null Clifford coefficients
  (`c^# = c`, `c^2 = 0`, `c1 c2 = g + b`, `c2 c1 = g - b`), every
  two-term null-soldered current `L = c1 A + c2 B` (coefficients
  commuting with the `#`-images of the transports) satisfies

    `L^# L = g (A^# B + B^# A) + b (A^# B - B^# A)`

  - no PSD diagonal survives (finding F2: squares in this calculus are
  shaped like `D^#D` itself). Corollary (`closure_current_square`,
  finding F4 direction): if `A^# B` is `#`-skew the aperture-shaped part
  cancels and `L^# L = 2 (b (A^# B))`; with `A^# B = -K/2` this is the
  memo's exact representation `Q_C = L^# L`. The concrete two-transport
  model instantiation is rung L4 (queued; carry the guard-pinned 4-slot
  normalization and the `#`-vs-dagger caveat verbatim).

## Claim boundary

Finite algebra only. L3 is stated with explicit hypotheses (the
anti-homomorphism and commutation data), matching the run's
explicit-hypothesis lemma style; no claim about which concrete carriers
satisfy them beyond the oracle-verified two-transport class. Positivity
is NOT claimed anywhere - Amendment B's whole point is that these squares
carry none (gate S1-CC owns that question).

## Provenance

S1 contribution memo 2026-07-07 (verified: identities re-derived by hand,
oracle reproduced 24/24, executor crosscheck N in {3,4,5} x random null
pairs x {SU(2), SU(3)}) - [comp]; SU(2) centrality is Cayley-Hamilton
folklore - [import].
-/

import Mathlib

namespace PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.GateYM.S1ClosureCurrentAlgebra

open Matrix
open scoped BigOperators

/-! ## L1: SU(2) centrality of the per-face closure Gram -/

/-- Generic 2x2 identity: `W + adjugate W = (tr W) . 1` (any commutative
scalar ring; stated over `C` for this lane). -/
theorem fin_two_add_adjugate (W : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ) :
    W + W.adjugate = W.trace • 1 := by
  rw [Matrix.adjugate_fin_two, Matrix.trace_fin_two]
  ext i j
  fin_cases i <;> fin_cases j <;> simp [add_comm]

/-- For special-unitary `W` (2x2, `W^H W = 1`, `det W = 1`), the
conjugate transpose IS the adjugate. -/
theorem conjTranspose_eq_adjugate_of_unitary_det_one
    (W : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ) (hU : Wᴴ * W = 1) (hdet : W.det = 1) :
    Wᴴ = W.adjugate := by
  have h2 : W * W.adjugate = 1 := by
    rw [Matrix.mul_adjugate, hdet, one_smul]
  calc Wᴴ = Wᴴ * (W * W.adjugate) := by rw [h2, mul_one]
    _ = (Wᴴ * W) * W.adjugate := by rw [mul_assoc]
    _ = W.adjugate := by rw [hU, one_mul]

/-- **L1 (SU(2) centrality).** The per-face closure Gram of a
special-unitary holonomy is central: `(1-W)(1-W)^H = (2 - tr W) . 1`.
Fails for SU(3) (oracle R1.3) - a pseudoreality luxury, not used by
L3/L4. -/
theorem su2_closure_gram_central (W : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ)
    (hU : Wᴴ * W = 1) (hdet : W.det = 1) :
    (1 - W) * (1 - W)ᴴ = (2 - W.trace) • 1 := by
  have hWWH : W * Wᴴ = 1 := mul_eq_one_comm.mp hU
  have hexp : (1 - W) * (1 - W)ᴴ = 1 - W - Wᴴ + W * Wᴴ := by
    rw [conjTranspose_sub, conjTranspose_one]
    noncomm_ring
  rw [hexp, hWWH, conjTranspose_eq_adjugate_of_unitary_det_one W hU hdet]
  calc (1 : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ) - W - W.adjugate + 1
      = (2 : ℂ) • (1 : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ) - (W + W.adjugate) := by
        rw [two_smul]
        noncomm_ring
    _ = (2 : ℂ) • (1 : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ) - W.trace • 1 := by
        rw [fin_two_add_adjugate]
    _ = (2 - W.trace) • 1 := by rw [sub_smul]

/-- The scalar evaluation: `2 - 2 cos t = 4 sin^2(t/2)` - the per-face
closure Gram is the same `1 - cos` obstruction as the two-edge aperture
mass (the "aperture rhyme" of finding F1). -/
theorem two_sub_two_cos (t : ℝ) :
    2 - 2 * Real.cos t = 4 * Real.sin (t / 2) ^ 2 := by
  have h := Real.cos_sq (t / 2)
  have ht : 2 * (t / 2) = t := by ring
  rw [ht] at h
  rw [Real.sin_sq, h]
  ring

/-! ## L2: the exact per-face split -/

/-- **L2 (per-face split, finding F1).** For any unitary `W`, the closure
defect splits exactly into a Hermitian Gram part and a skew
field-strength part: `2 (1 - W) = (1-W)(1-W)^H + (W^H - W)`. The Gram
part is the energy-shaped (`a^4 |F|^2`) object of Amendment A0; the skew
part is the field-strength (`a^2 F`) object whose commutator descendant
is the Weitzenboeck `Q_C` slot - the two must never be conflated
(Amendment B wording rail). -/
theorem closure_defect_split {n : Type*} [Fintype n] [DecidableEq n]
    (W : Matrix n n ℂ) (hU : Wᴴ * W = 1) :
    (2 : ℂ) • ((1 : Matrix n n ℂ) - W)
      = (1 - W) * (1 - W)ᴴ + (Wᴴ - W) := by
  have hWWH : W * Wᴴ = 1 := mul_eq_one_comm.mp hU
  have hexp : (1 - W) * (1 - W)ᴴ = 1 - W - Wᴴ + W * Wᴴ := by
    rw [conjTranspose_sub, conjTranspose_one]
    noncomm_ring
  rw [hexp, hWWH, two_smul]
  noncomm_ring

/-! ## L3: the null-soldered square lemma (abstract) -/

section NullSolderedSquare

variable {R : Type*} [Ring R]

/-- **L3 (null-soldered square, finding F2).** In any ring with an
additive, multiplication-reversing map `s` ("the Krein adjoint") fixing
the two null Clifford coefficients, the square of a two-term
null-soldered current has NO diagonal part: it is an aperture-shaped
symmetric term plus a bivector-shaped antisymmetric term,

  `s(L) L = g (s(A) B + s(B) A) + b (s(A) B - s(B) A)`,

where `c1 c2 = g + b` and `c2 c1 = g - b`. Hypotheses are explicit (run
lemma style); the commutation data says the Clifford coefficients act on
a different tensor factor than the transports. -/
theorem null_soldered_square (s : R → R)
    (hadd : ∀ x y : R, s (x + y) = s x + s y)
    (hmul : ∀ x y : R, s (x * y) = s y * s x)
    (c₁ c₂ A B g b : R)
    (hs1 : s c₁ = c₁) (hs2 : s c₂ = c₂)
    (hc1 : c₁ * c₁ = 0) (hc2 : c₂ * c₂ = 0)
    (h12 : c₁ * c₂ = g + b) (h21 : c₂ * c₁ = g - b)
    (hA1 : Commute c₁ (s A)) (hA2 : Commute c₂ (s A))
    (hB1 : Commute c₁ (s B)) (hB2 : Commute c₂ (s B)) :
    s (c₁ * A + c₂ * B) * (c₁ * A + c₂ * B)
      = g * (s A * B + s B * A) + b * (s A * B - s B * A) := by
  have hsL : s (c₁ * A + c₂ * B) = s A * c₁ + s B * c₂ := by
    rw [hadd, hmul, hmul, hs1, hs2]
  have hA12 : Commute (s A) (c₁ * c₂) := (hA1.symm).mul_right (hA2.symm)
  have hB21 : Commute (s B) (c₂ * c₁) := (hB2.symm).mul_right (hB1.symm)
  have t1 : s A * c₁ * (c₁ * A) = 0 := by
    rw [mul_assoc, ← mul_assoc c₁ c₁ A, hc1, zero_mul, mul_zero]
  have t4 : s B * c₂ * (c₂ * B) = 0 := by
    rw [mul_assoc, ← mul_assoc c₂ c₂ B, hc2, zero_mul, mul_zero]
  have t2 : s A * c₁ * (c₂ * B) = (g + b) * (s A * B) := by
    calc s A * c₁ * (c₂ * B) = s A * (c₁ * c₂) * B := by
          rw [mul_assoc, mul_assoc, mul_assoc]
      _ = (c₁ * c₂) * s A * B := by rw [hA12.eq]
      _ = (g + b) * (s A * B) := by rw [h12, mul_assoc]
  have t3 : s B * c₂ * (c₁ * A) = (g - b) * (s B * A) := by
    calc s B * c₂ * (c₁ * A) = s B * (c₂ * c₁) * A := by
          rw [mul_assoc, mul_assoc, mul_assoc]
      _ = (c₂ * c₁) * s B * A := by rw [hB21.eq]
      _ = (g - b) * (s B * A) := by rw [h21, mul_assoc]
  rw [hsL, add_mul, mul_add, mul_add, t1, t2, t3, t4]
  noncomm_ring

/-- **Closure-current corollary (finding F4 direction).** If the
transport pairing `s(A) B` is `s`-skew (`s(B) A = -(s(A) B)`), the
aperture-shaped part cancels and the square is purely bivector:
`s(L) L = 2 (b (s(A) B))`. Instantiating `s(A) B = -K/2` on the
  two-transport carrier gives the memo's exact representation
  `Q_C = L^# L` - that instantiation is rung L4, queued. -/
theorem closure_current_square (s : R → R)
    (hadd : ∀ x y : R, s (x + y) = s x + s y)
    (hmul : ∀ x y : R, s (x * y) = s y * s x)
    (c₁ c₂ A B g b : R)
    (hs1 : s c₁ = c₁) (hs2 : s c₂ = c₂)
    (hc1 : c₁ * c₁ = 0) (hc2 : c₂ * c₂ = 0)
    (h12 : c₁ * c₂ = g + b) (h21 : c₂ * c₁ = g - b)
    (hA1 : Commute c₁ (s A)) (hA2 : Commute c₂ (s A))
    (hB1 : Commute c₁ (s B)) (hB2 : Commute c₂ (s B))
    (hskew : s B * A = -(s A * B)) :
    s (c₁ * A + c₂ * B) * (c₁ * A + c₂ * B)
      = 2 • (b * (s A * B)) := by
  rw [null_soldered_square s hadd hmul c₁ c₂ A B g b hs1 hs2 hc1 hc2
    h12 h21 hA1 hA2 hB1 hB2, hskew]
  rw [add_neg_cancel, mul_zero, zero_add, sub_neg_eq_add, two_smul,
    mul_add]

/-- **K2/L4 stabilized finite-product form.**  A family of two-direction
closure currents squares componentwise in the finite pair-indexed target.  This
is the algebraic core of the pair-stabilized direct-sum route: the target keeps
one component for each direction pair, so no cross-pair terms are present.

Claim boundary: this is still abstract finite algebra over an explicit
pointwise product ring.  It is not a positivity theorem, and it does not identify
the component sum with a concrete carrier's `Q_C` until a separate
normalization/instantiation theorem supplies the pair index and signs. -/
theorem closure_current_square_pi {ι : Type*} (s : R → R)
    (hadd : ∀ x y : R, s (x + y) = s x + s y)
    (hmul : ∀ x y : R, s (x * y) = s y * s x)
    (c₁ c₂ A B g b : ι → R)
    (hs1 : ∀ i, s (c₁ i) = c₁ i) (hs2 : ∀ i, s (c₂ i) = c₂ i)
    (hc1 : ∀ i, c₁ i * c₁ i = 0) (hc2 : ∀ i, c₂ i * c₂ i = 0)
    (h12 : ∀ i, c₁ i * c₂ i = g i + b i)
    (h21 : ∀ i, c₂ i * c₁ i = g i - b i)
    (hA1 : ∀ i, Commute (c₁ i) (s (A i)))
    (hA2 : ∀ i, Commute (c₂ i) (s (A i)))
    (hB1 : ∀ i, Commute (c₁ i) (s (B i)))
    (hB2 : ∀ i, Commute (c₂ i) (s (B i)))
    (hskew : ∀ i, s (B i) * A i = -(s (A i) * B i)) :
    (fun i => s (c₁ i * A i + c₂ i * B i))
        * (fun i => c₁ i * A i + c₂ i * B i)
      = fun i => 2 • (b i * (s (A i) * B i)) := by
  funext i
  exact closure_current_square s hadd hmul (c₁ i) (c₂ i) (A i) (B i) (g i) (b i)
    (hs1 i) (hs2 i) (hc1 i) (hc2 i) (h12 i) (h21 i)
    (hA1 i) (hA2 i) (hB1 i) (hB2 i) (hskew i)

end NullSolderedSquare

/-! ## Abstract finite-product form layer for K2 direct-sum stabilization -/

noncomputable section FiniteProductForm

variable {ι V R W : Type*} [Fintype ι] [DecidableEq ι]
variable [CommSemiring R] [AddCommMonoid V] [Module R V]

/-- Include one finite-product component into the product target.

This is the algebraic placeholder for a pair block in the K2 direct-sum route.
It does not choose the physical pair index, signs, or carrier normalization. -/
def componentInclusion (i : ι) : V →ₗ[R] (ι → V) where
  toFun v := Pi.single i v
  map_add' := by
    intro v w
    ext j
    by_cases h : j = i
    · subst j
      simp
    · simp [Pi.single_eq_of_ne h]
  map_smul' := by
    intro a v
    ext j
    by_cases h : j = i
    · subst j
      simp
    · simp [Pi.single_eq_of_ne h]

/-- The finite orthogonal-product bilinear form with component forms `β i`.

For K2 this is the carrier-abstract form-level object whose off-diagonal
orthogonality is proved below. It is not a positivity structure and is not yet
the concrete null-edge carrier's Krein form. -/
abbrev finiteProductForm (β : ι → LinearMap.BilinForm R V) :
    LinearMap.BilinForm R (ι → V) where
  toFun x := {
    toFun := fun y => ∑ i, β i (x i) (y i)
    map_add' := by
      intro y z
      simp [Pi.add_apply, map_add, Finset.sum_add_distrib]
    map_smul' := by
      intro a y
      simp [Pi.smul_apply, map_smul, Finset.mul_sum] }
  map_add' := by
    intro x y
    ext z
    simp [Pi.add_apply, map_add, Finset.sum_add_distrib]
  map_smul' := by
    intro a x
    ext z
    simp [Pi.smul_apply, map_smul, Finset.mul_sum]

/-- A component inclusion is isometric for the matching component form.

This is a finite-product form identity only; it does not identify the component
with a physical closure-current block. -/
theorem finiteProductForm_component_same
    (β : ι → LinearMap.BilinForm R V) (i : ι) (v w : V) :
    finiteProductForm β ((componentInclusion (R := R) i) v)
        ((componentInclusion (R := R) i) w) = β i v w := by
  classical
  simp only [finiteProductForm, componentInclusion, LinearMap.coe_mk, AddHom.coe_mk]
  rw [Finset.sum_eq_single i]
  · simp
  · intro j _ hji
    simp [Pi.single_eq_of_ne hji]
  · intro hi
    exact False.elim (hi (Finset.mem_univ i))

/-- Distinct component inclusions are orthogonal for `finiteProductForm`.

This is the precise no-cross-term algebraic fact needed before the K2
pair-stabilized direct-sum route can be instantiated on a concrete carrier. -/
theorem finiteProductForm_component_ne
    (β : ι → LinearMap.BilinForm R V) {i j : ι} (hij : i ≠ j) (v w : V) :
    finiteProductForm β ((componentInclusion (R := R) i) v)
        ((componentInclusion (R := R) j) w) = 0 := by
  classical
  simp only [finiteProductForm, componentInclusion, LinearMap.coe_mk, AddHom.coe_mk]
  apply Finset.sum_eq_zero
  intro k _
  by_cases hki : k = i
  · subst k
    simp [Pi.single_eq_of_ne hij]
  · simp [Pi.single_eq_of_ne hki]

/-- Evaluating the finite-product form on an assembled family is the sum of
component evaluations.

This is the abstract form-level version of "the square of the assembled
pair-current is the sum of pair squares" once the carrier supplies the actual
component forms and maps. -/
theorem finiteProductForm_assemble_eval
    (β : ι → LinearMap.BilinForm R V) (L : ι → W → V) (x y : W) :
    finiteProductForm β (fun i => L i x) (fun i => L i y)
      = ∑ i, β i (L i x) (L i y) := by
  rfl

end FiniteProductForm

end PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.GateYM.S1ClosureCurrentAlgebra

```

### PhysicsSM/Draft/NullEdge/Carrier/ChiralZeroModeParity.lean (113 lines)

```lean
/-
# Chiral-symmetry forcing of protected transfer eigenvalues (T1)

DRAFT (kernel-clean; no `s o r r y`). The K6 target of the overnight
all-mass run (2026-07-08), redirected by the determinant-parity probe
`Scripts/oracle/p1_zeromode_symmetry_invariant.py`.

## What the probe decided (numeric oracle; guides this statement)

The protected unit eigenvalue of the decorated-cycle transfer `W` is NOT
forced by cyclic symmetry - abstract winding-1 symmetric data is UNPINNED
(min|ev - 1| ~ 0.2-0.45), and equivariant-but-unpinned cases are common.
It is forced by a CHIRAL symmetry: an involution `Gamma` (`Gamma^2 = 1`)
with `Gamma W Gamma = W^dagger`. Numerically this `Gamma` is the
orientation-swap, i.e. the edge-orientation-reversal grading - the SAME
grading that gives the exact Ginsparg-Wilson structure
(`GWRetardedTransfer.lean`). It is present exactly for the even-V
half-winding (alternating-phase) decoration and there pins BOTH `+1` and
`-1` in `spec(W)` for every hop amplitude `|t|` (probe: V = 4, 6, 8).

## What this module proves (the honest kernel core)

For a unitary `W` carrying such a chiral involution:

* `chiral_det_conj`: `det W` is real (`conj (det W) = det W`);
* `chiral_det_sq_one`: `(det W)^2 = 1`;
* `chiral_det_eq_pm_one`: hence `det W = 1` or `det W = -1`.

Since `det W = (-1)^{mult(-1)}` up to conjugate-pair factors, the sign of
`det W` fixes the PARITY of the `-1`-eigenvalue multiplicity - the honest
core of the determinant-parity mechanism (SevenChallenges memo finding 8).
The full `|t|`-independent DOUBLE pinning (both `+-1` always present at
even dimension) needs the chiral winding invariant (Asboth-Obuse `Z x Z`
for chiral discrete-time walks, arXiv:1303.1199) and is left at oracle

## Provenance

`Gamma U Gamma = U^dagger` structure and its `0/pi`-mode consequences are

import Mathlib

namespace PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.ChiralZeroModeParity

open Matrix

variable {n : Type*} [Fintype n] [DecidableEq n]

/-- A **chiral involution** for `W`: a self-inverse `Gamma` conjugating `W`
to its adjoint. For unitary `W` (`Wᴴ * W = 1`) this says `Gamma` conjugates
`W` to `W⁻¹` - the reciprocal-pairing (chiral) symmetry. -/
structure ChiralInvolution (W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ) : Prop where
  invol : Gamma * Gamma = 1
  chiral : Gamma * W * Gamma = Wᴴ

/-- Under a chiral involution, `det Gamma` squares to one. -/
theorem chiral_detGamma_sq {W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ}
    (h : ChiralInvolution W Gamma) : Gamma.det * Gamma.det = 1 := by
  rw [← Matrix.det_mul, h.invol, Matrix.det_one]

/-- **`det W` is real** under a chiral involution: `conj (det W) = det W`.
(Taking `det` of `Gamma W Gamma = Wᴴ` and using `det Gamma ^2 = 1`.) -/
theorem chiral_det_conj {W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ}
    (h : ChiralInvolution W Gamma) :
    (starRingEnd ℂ) W.det = W.det := by
  have hdet : Gamma.det * W.det * Gamma.det = (Wᴴ).det := by
    rw [← Matrix.det_mul, ← Matrix.det_mul, h.chiral]
  rw [Matrix.det_conjTranspose] at hdet
  -- LHS = det W * (det Gamma)^2 = det W
  have hcomm : Gamma.det * W.det * Gamma.det
      = W.det * (Gamma.det * Gamma.det) := by ring
  rw [hcomm, chiral_detGamma_sq h, mul_one] at hdet
  exact hdet.symm

/-- Under a chiral involution with `W` unitary, `(det W)^2 = 1`. -/
theorem chiral_det_sq_one {W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ}
    (h : ChiralInvolution W Gamma) (hU : Wᴴ * W = 1) :
    W.det * W.det = 1 := by
  -- |det W|^2 = 1 from unitarity, and det W real from the chiral symmetry
  have hmod : (starRingEnd ℂ) W.det * W.det = 1 := by
    have : (Wᴴ * W).det = 1 := by rw [hU, Matrix.det_one]
    rwa [Matrix.det_mul, Matrix.det_conjTranspose] at this
  rw [chiral_det_conj h] at hmod
  exact hmod

/-- **The chiral determinant dichotomy (T1 core).** A unitary `W` carrying
a chiral involution has `det W = 1` or `det W = -1`. The sign fixes the
parity of the `-1`-eigenvalue multiplicity: the honest kernel core of the
determinant-parity forcing of protected `+-1` transfer modes. -/
theorem chiral_det_eq_pm_one {W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ}
    (h : ChiralInvolution W Gamma) (hU : Wᴴ * W = 1) :
    W.det = 1 ∨ W.det = -1 := by
  have hsq : W.det * W.det = 1 := chiral_det_sq_one h hU
  have : (W.det - 1) * (W.det + 1) = 0 := by
    have : (W.det - 1) * (W.det + 1) = W.det * W.det - 1 := by ring
    rw [this, hsq, sub_self]
  rcases mul_eq_zero.mp this with h1 | h2
  · exact Or.inl (by linear_combination h1)
  · exact Or.inr (by linear_combination h2)

end PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.ChiralZeroModeParity

## Part B — the chosen crux: **S1-CC**, resolved as a structured no-go with content

I choose crux 1. I claim I can (a) give the principled finite `V'`, (b) settle descent with an exact iff, and (c) **predict the restricted inertia in closed form: exactly balanced**. The answer reframes the gate: the closure channel is *honestly signed on the physical sector*, the balance is structural (a grading anticonjugation), and the residual positivity question relocates — in a way that converges exactly with the repo's own `p > q` MEMO note in `KreinPositiveSectorWitness.lean`.

### B.0 The transcription (fixing notation, matching the repo's shapes)

Two-direction closure factor. Null pair `c₁, c₂` with `c₁² = c₂² = 0`, `c₁c₂ + c₂c₁ = 2g·1`, `g ≠ 0` (non-collinear; rescale `g = 1`). Concrete model **[MEMO]**: `H = ℂ² ⊗ W`, `W` a Hilbert space (sites/color),

```text
c₁ = √2 E₀₁ ⊗ 1,   c₂ = √2 E₁₀ ⊗ 1,   J = σ_x ⊗ 1,   b = (c₁c₂ − c₂c₁)/2 = σ_z ⊗ 1
```

so `c_i^# = J c_i^† J = c_i` ✓, and the repo's two-direction closure slot is `Q_C ∝ b ⊗ K`, `K = [∇₁,∇₂]`, `K^† = −K` (matching `banks_casher_count`'s skew-Hermitian convention). **Consistency check** [MEMO]: `J·Q_C = (σ_xσ_z) ⊗ K` has spectrum `{±λ_j}` symmetric with multiplicity — this *reproduces* the program's own "sig(J Q_C) maximally indefinite on the full space" (Amendment B) as the `G = 0` special case of Theorem 2 below. My model is the right transcription.

### B.1 (a) The principled finite `V'` — and why its shape is *forced*

**Lemma 1 (half-constraint rigidity). [MEMO / M-target]** Let `Q = c₁ ⊗ G₁ + c₂ ⊗ G₂`. Then `Q² = (c₁c₂) ⊗ G₁G₂ + (c₂c₁) ⊗ G₂G₁`, and since `c₁c₂ = 2E₀₀`, `c₂c₁ = 2E₁₁` are linearly independent, `Q² = 0 ⟺ G₁G₂ = 0 ∧ G₂G₁ = 0`. So for any Gauss operator that is not two-sided-annihilating, **the nilpotent constraint charge must use a single null covector**. The Gupta-Bleuler "impose only half the constraint" is not a choice on the hyperbolic pair — it is forced by nilpotency. *(Lean: four-line matrix algebra.)*

**Definition (the Gauss charge and sector). [M-target]**

```text
Q_G := c(α₁) ⊗ G,   G = G† the finite Gauss operator on W,
V'  := ker Q_G = (e₀ ⊗ W) ⊕ (e₁ ⊗ ker G),
N   := radical of [·,·] on V' = e₀ ⊗ range G = range Q_G.
```

`Q_G² = 0` for **any** `G` (Clifford nilpotency); `Q_G^# = Q_G ⟺ G† = G` [MEMO]. The identification `N = range Q_G` is exactly `finite_kugo_ojima` instantiated — **already M in the repo**; the model plugs straight into the Q01 interface. For the concrete carrier, `G` is the linearized lattice divergence (generator of the gauge action at vertices) — abelian/linearized case first, per the roadmap's own "one-day numeric probe" scoping.

Explicitly: `V'/N ≅ (e₀ ⊗ ker G) ⊕ (e₁ ⊗ ker G)`, and the induced Krein form is the **hyperbolic pairing between the two copies of `ker G`** [MEMO — computed: `[e₀⊗w, e₁⊗v] = ⟨w,v⟩`, both diagonal blocks isotropic].

### B.2 (b) Descent, settled with an exact iff

**Theorem 1 (descent ⟺ finite Ward). [MEMO / M-target]**
`Q_C(V') ⊆ V'` and `Q_C(N) ⊆ N` **iff** `K(ker G) ⊆ ker G`. Sufficient: `[G, K] = 0` (gauge covariance of curvature — exact in the abelian/linearized case). Moreover the backward implication `K(ker G) ⊆ ker G ⟹ K(range G) ⊆ range G` is free from `K† = −K` (`range G = (ker G)^⊥` for Hermitian `G`). So descent is a single subspace-invariance condition, and it is the finite Ward identity.

**Correction to the gate as posed.** S1-CC asks whether "the GL-torsor meets the constraint-compatible operators (`L_A V' ⊆ V'`)". Computed: `L_A(e₀⊗w) = √2 e₁ ⊗ Bw`, so `L_A V' ⊆ V'` requires `range B ⊆ ker G`; for torsor members (`A` invertible, `B = (A†)⁻¹(−K/2)`) with `K` nondegenerate this **fails for every representative** [MEMO]. But this does not matter: **positivity descent never needed the current to descend — only the square `Q_C` must descend, and it does (Theorem 1).** The gate conflated the two. This is the reframing half of my answer.

### B.3 (c) The restricted inertia — closed form, and a structural no-go

**Theorem 2 (restricted inertia). [MEMO / M-target]** On `V'/N ≅ ker G ⊕ ker G` with the hyperbolic induced form, the closure form is the Hermitian block matrix `[[0, ∓K̄],[±K̄, 0]]` with `K̄ = K|_{ker G}` — quadratic form `∓2 Re⟨w, K̄ v⟩` — with inertia

```text
( rank K̄ , rank K̄ , 2(dim ker G − rank K̄) ).
```

**Exactly balanced. Closure positivity on the physical sector holds only vacuously (`K̄ = 0`, flat Gauss sector); otherwise the channel is honestly signed with signature zero.**

**Theorem 3 (why — the grading anticonjugation no-go). [M-target; this is the Lean flagship]** Let `S` be invertible with `S† (J'Q') S = −(J'Q')`. Then `n₊ = n₋` (Sylvester: inertia is a congruence invariant, and `J'Q' ≅ −J'Q'`). Instantiate `S = b̄ = σ_z ⊗ 1`: it preserves `V'` and `N` (so it descends) and anticonjugates `J`, because `Jb = −bJ`. Hence **every `V'` invariant under the closure bivector grading has balanced closure inertia** — and every gauge-defined constraint is `b`-invariant, because gauge acts on `W` alone. A positive closure sector would require a constraint that *mixes the Clifford factor with the color factor*, which Lemma 1 shows cannot be a nilpotent charge on the pair.

**The house-style Lean rung (recommended first landing).** Avoid inertia API entirely; land the trace form first:

```lean
theorem anticonj_odd_pow_trace_zero {n} [Fintype n] [DecidableEq n]
    (B S : Matrix n n ℂ) (hS : IsUnit S) (h : S⁻¹ * B * S = -B) (k : ℕ) :
    (B ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace = 0
```

Proof: `tr B^(2k+1) = tr (S⁻¹ B^(2k+1) S) = tr ((−B)^(2k+1)) = −tr B^(2k+1)`. Pure ring algebra plus `Matrix.trace_mul_comm` — the same "finite trace identity, spectral-rail safe" register as `banks_casher_count`. Then the inertia reading via `Matrix.charpoly_units_conj` (similar matrices, equal charpoly, `B ∼ −B` ⟹ spectrum symmetric under negation) and `Matrix.IsHermitian.eigenvalues`; Sylvester is available if wanted via `QuadraticForm.equivalent_signType_weighted_sum_squared` and friends. Plus a concrete non-vacuous witness, fully explicit at 6×6: `W = ℂ³`, `G = diag(0,0,1)`, `K = antisym(e₀,e₁)` — `[G,K] = 0`, `dim V' = 5`, `dim N = 1`, quotient inertia `(2,2,0)` [MEMO, computed].

### B.4 The trade-off with total-operator positivity — the honest relocation

The same anticonjugation kills more than closure: on the hyperbolic-pair factor, `J·(1⊗X)` and `J·(b⊗X)` are *both* flipped by `S = b`, so **every channel built over the pair alone is balanced on `V'/N`** [MEMO]. Positive physical norm can therefore *never* be sourced inside the closure doublet — it must come from the `J`-definite complement of the doublet (transverse/matter directions of the full carrier), exactly as in Gupta-Bleuler the scalar-longitudinal pair contributes zero and the photon's norm lives transversely. This converges precisely with the repo's `(2,1)` vs `(1,2)` witnesses: **the decision quantity is the ambient inertia surplus `p − q` on the doublet-free complement** — the `p > q` nonvacuity MEMO of `KreinPositiveSectorWitness.lean`, now with a mechanism. Note also the exact dichotomy the model exhibits [MEMO]: on `range G` (constraint effective) the doublet is eaten by the quotient — Gupta-Bleuler cancellation; on `ker G` (gauge-invariant content) the doublet survives with hyperbolic signature — which is the honest indefiniteness of the Dirac adjoint form `ψ̄ψ` on physical states, i.e. chirality. §6 and §8 of the manuscript are coupled even more tightly than stated: the balanced closure spectrum on the Gauss sector *is* the finite chromomagnetic equioscillation (hyperfine: Δ up, N down — De Rújula–Georgi–Glashow **[import]**), and it is what lets curvature pull total-operator eigenvalues toward zero from both sides, feeding the Banks-Casher count. **Grade C, pre-registered:** total-operator positivity on the full quotient holds iff an aperture/turn-dominance inequality over the definite complement holds (Weyl-type bound: definite-block minimum ≥ spectral radius of the doublet cross-coupling); numeric probe alongside the inertia probe.

### B.5 Kill conditions (pre-registered)

1. **K-A (identification kill):** transcribe the concrete plaquette carrier's Gauss covectors. If the natural `G` is *not* of the form `1_{ℂ²} ⊗ G` (i.e. genuinely soldered, mixing Clifford and color factors), the `b`-invariance hypothesis of Theorem 3 fails and my inertia prediction is void (Lemma 1 then says the constraint cannot be a nilpotent charge, forcing the ghost-extended BRST route). 
2. **K-B (numeric kill):** the roadmap's one-day probe, run on the transcribed `V'`: prediction `sig(J Q_C|_{V'/N}) = (rank K̄, rank K̄)`, and `= (2,2)` on the 6×6 witness. Any *unbalanced non-vacuous* result kills the identification (the abstract Theorems 1–3 survive; their instantiation dies).
3. **K-C (nonabelian kill):** if on the nonabelian carrier no Hermitian `G` implementing the Gauss covectors satisfies `K(ker G) ⊆ ker G` with `ker G ≠ 0`, Theorem 1's hypothesis is empty there and S1-CC must be re-posed at the linearized level only.

### B.6 Summary of the crux resolution

S1-CC closes as: **no, closure is not positive on the physical sector — it is exactly balanced, structurally, and vacuously positive only on flat sectors; the gate's torsor-compatibility question was the wrong question (only the square needs to descend, and descent ⟺ the finite Ward condition); and the surviving positivity question is the ambient inertia surplus `p − q` on the doublet-free complement.** A sharp no-go with a mechanism, three M-target theorems (Lemma 1, Theorems 1–3), a one-afternoon trace-identity Lean rung in house style, a 6×6 explicit witness, and three kill conditions. I recommend formalizing in the order: `anticonj_odd_pow_trace_zero` → Lemma 1 → the 6×6 witness (descent + balanced inertia by `decide`-style computation) → the abstract Theorems 1–2 over the Q01 `finite_kugo_ojima` interface.

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016H2AdXXA4dxqyNKqhmvEGm","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# The null-edge mass program: status and hardest challenges\n2\t\n3\tA standalone brief for external collaborators, 2026-07-08. No access to\n4\tour repository is assumed; everything needed is defined here. We are\n5\tasking for guidance on the seven challenges in section 5. Please read\n6\tsection 6 (pitfalls and closed routes) before proposing approaches -\n7\tseveral natural ideas are already dead by proof or measurement.\n8\t\n9\t## 1. What this project is\n10\t\n11\tA machine-verified physics program: we formalize, in Lean 4 (Mathlib,\n12\tpinned toolchain), the thesis that **mass is the obstruction to coherent\n13\tnull transport** - matter is trapped, mutually disagreeing light. The\n14\tLean kernel is our source of truth. Every claim carries a grade:\n15\t**T** (source-verified theorem), **M** (machine/kernel-verified),\n16\t**MEMO** (expert-verified prose, kernel transcription pending),\n17\t**C** (pre-registered conjecture with explicit kill conditions),\n18\t**[import]** (external input). We pre-register tests before running\n19\tthem, and we report kills as loudly as wins - two long-standing\n20\tconjectures died this week by their own pre-registered criteria, and we\n21\tcount that as the system working.\n22\t\n23\t## 2. The trusted core (kernel-checked, no caveats)\n24\t\n25\tFor 2-component Weyl spinors `psi_1..psi_n` (null momenta\n26\t`psi_i psi_i^dag`), with `P = sum_i psi_i psi_i^dag`:\n27\t\n28\t```text\n29\tdet P = sum_{i<j} |psi_i wedge psi_j|^2\n30\t```\n31\t\n32\tInvariant mass squared IS total pairwise null-direction disagreement;\n33\tzero iff all directions collinear. Machine-checked, no axioms beyond\n34\tthe standard three. Everything else orbits this.\n35\t\n36\t## 3. The formal architecture (one paragraph each)\n37\t\n38\t**Carrier.** A finite 2-complex (vertices, edges, faces) carrying a\n39\tDirac-type operator `D = sum_e c(alpha_e) nabla_e + Gamma phi`: null\n40\tcovector solderings `alpha_e` (Clifford coefficients `c(alpha)^2 = 0`),\n41\tcovariant edge transports `nabla_e`, and a vertex \"turn\" term (the\n42\tHiggs-shaped channel). A kernel-checked Weitzenboeck identity splits\n43\t`4 D^#D` into four channels: aperture `Q_A` (kinetic, ties to det P),\n44\tclosure `Q_C` (gauge/QCD-shaped: commutators of transports), turn `Q_T`\n45\t(Yukawa-shaped), soldering-gradient `E` (gravity-shaped). Slogan:\n46\tunification is decomposition. `#` is a Krein-space adjoint - the\n47\tgeometry is indefinite by construction, and positivity holds (at best)\n48\tonly on a constrained physical sector, Gupta-Bleuler style.\n49\t\n50\t**Protection.** Finite McKean-Singer index theorems (kernel-checked):\n51\tan unbalanced chirality count forces exact massless modes immune to\n52\tevery potential and transport.\n53\t\n54\t**Dynamics.** Kernel-checked this week: one decimation (Schur\n55\tcomplement) step converts per-edge null structure into non-null\n56\teffective terms with coefficient exactly the pairwise Clifford pairing\n57\t(`(ab)^2 = k(ab)` for square-zero a, b with anticommutator k); the\n58\teffective coupling vanishes identically for collinear pairs. So the\n59\tSAME disagreement invariant that is mass kinematically becomes mass\n60\tdynamically under coarse-graining.\n61\t\n62\t**QCD lane.** Kernel-checked at strong coupling on concrete finite\n63\tlattices: Wilson-loop area law, slab reflection positivity, a\n64\ttransfer/OS spectral gap, exponential clustering, character-expansion\n65\tbounds. Also: the Wilson plaquette action is EXACTLY the squared\n66\tHilbert-Schmidt norm of the closure defect `1 - holonomy` (one-line\n67\tidentity, now pinned). This week's sharpest structural result (verified\n68\tanalytically + by two independent numerical oracles, Lean transcription\n69\tin progress): the nonabelian closure channel is an EXACT Krein square,\n70\t`Q_C = L^# L`, with an explicit gauge-covariant current\n71\t`L = c(alpha_1) (x) 1 + c(alpha_2) (x) (-K/2)`, `K = [nabla_1,nabla_2]`,\n72\tfor ANY compact group - but Krein squares carry no positivity by\n73\tthemselves, which relocates the whole positivity question (challenge\n74\tC2).\n75\t\n76\t## 4. Status snapshot (end of 2026-07-07)\n77\t\n78\t- Kernel-checked: the section-2 theorem; the 4-channel Weitzenboeck\n79\t  split; index-protection family; the decimation/mass-generation\n80\t  witness with its collinear control; the strong-coupling QCD stack\n81\t  above; exact finite Ginsparg-Wilson chirality structure whose grading\n82\t  is edge-orientation reversal; a nonvacuous positive Gupta-Bleuler\n83\t  quotient witness on a small model; color-fiber structure (particle\n84\t  states as exterior-algebra \"strand\" monomials; hypercharge derived;\n85\t  lepton/baryon number = strand-count grading).\n86\t- MEMO grade: Standard-Model representation selection (five-strand\n87\t  principle); three generations via triality-as-monodromy (multiplicity\n88\t  menu {1,3} forced); equipartition trace identity behind the Koide\n89\t  combination.\n90\t- KILLED this week (pre-registered probes): see section 6.\n91\t- DISCOVERED this week: (i) every cyclically symmetric celestial\n92\t  decoration of a transport cycle pins an EXACT zero quasi-energy mode\n93\t  (generic decorations sit near zero); geometric decorations self-lock\n94\t  onto the abstract \"massless locus\"; (ii) the closure channel is\n95\t  chromomagnetic (linear in field strength), NOT energy-shaped - see\n96\t  pitfall P2.\n97\t\n98\t## 5. The hardest challenges (the ask)\n99\t\n100\t**C1 - a finite forest-counting injection (pure combinatorics; our\n101\tsingle most-resisted target: five failed expert-prover attempts).**\n102\tSetting: cluster/polymer expansion (Penrose-style tree partition\n103\tscheme). We need: for a fixed rooted-forest target with given block\n104\tsizes `m_j` summing appropriately, an explicit injection from\n105\t{fiber element} x Perm(k) x prod_j Perm(m_j) into orderings of n\n106\tlabeled objects (root-first, blocks in sigma-order, internally\n107\treordered), with fiber constancy - the missing bound is\n108\t`|fiber| * k! * prod m_j! <= n!`. The intended proof is a left-inverse\n109\t(parse the ordering back), with canonical-least-root and\n110\tincreasing-children constraints as tie-breaks. GUIDANCE SOUGHT: a\n111\tclean certificate/encoding argument, or a reference where exactly this\n112\tfiber bound is proved (Penrose 1967 descendants; Fernandez-Procacci),\n113\tor a reformulation that avoids the injection entirely.\n114\t\n115\t**C2 - constraint-compatible representatives in a Krein space (the\n116\tpositivity crux).** The closure channel is an exact Krein square\n117\t`Q_C = L^# L` whose representation family is a GL-torsor\n118\t{`L_A = c(alpha_1)(x)A + c(alpha_2)(x)(A^dag)^{-1}(-K/2)`}. On the FULL\n119\tspace the form `[psi, Q_C psi]` is maximally indefinite (necessarily -\n120\tnull Clifford coefficients are #-isotropic). Positivity can only hold\n121\ton a physical sector `V'/N` (Gauss-law kernel modulo null vectors), and\n122\tdescends IF some torsor member preserves `V'` and its constraint\n123\tsubspace. QUESTION: for lattice gauge Gauss constraints, is there a\n124\tprincipled construction of a constraint-compatible representative\n125\t(e.g. via gauge averaging, BRST-style doublets, or a positive-definite\n126\tdeformation)? Any literature on \"operator squares in Krein spaces\n127\trestricted to Pontryagin-positive sectors\"? A no-go would also be\n128\tvaluable: if the restricted form must stay indefinite, chiral symmetry\n129\tbreaking wants it that way (the curvature term must pull eigenvalues\n130\tdown), and we need the sharp statement of that trade-off.\n131\t\n132\t**C3 - multi-direction closure representation.** The exact square above\n133\tis proved for TWO transport directions. With d >= 3 directions,\n134\t`Q_C = -sum_{mu<nu} b_{mu nu} (x) [nabla_mu, nabla_nu]` and per-pair\n135\tcurrents generate cross-pair symmetric contaminations. No access to\nour repository is assumed; everything needed is defined here. We are\nasking for guidance on the seven challenges in section 5. Please read\nsection 6 (pitfalls and closed routes) before proposing approaches -\nseveral natural ideas are already dead by proof or measurement.\n\n## 1. What this project is\n\nA machine-verified physics program: we formalize, in Lean 4 (Mathlib,\npinned toolchain), the thesis that **mass is the obstruction to coherent\nnull transport** - matter is trapped, mutually disagreeing light. The\nLean kernel is our source of truth. Every claim carries a grade:\n**T** (source-verified theorem), **M** (machine/kernel-verified),\n**MEMO** (expert-verified prose, kernel transcription pending),\n**C** (pre-registered conjecture with explicit kill conditions),\n**[import]** (external input). We pre-register tests before running\nthem, and we report kills as loudly as wins - two long-standing\nconjectures died this week by their own pre-registered criteria, and we\ncount that as the system working.\n\n## 2. The trusted core (kernel-checked, no caveats)\n\nFor 2-component Weyl spinors `psi_1..psi_n` (null momenta\n`psi_i psi_i^dag`), with `P = sum_i psi_i psi_i^dag`:\n\n```text\ndet P = sum_{i<j} |psi_i wedge psi_j|^2\n```\n\nInvariant mass squared IS total pairwise null-direction disagreement;\nzero iff all directions collinear. Machine-checked, no axioms beyond\nthe standard three. Everything else orbits this.\n\n## 3. The formal architecture (one paragraph each)\n\n**Carrier.** A finite 2-complex (vertices, edges, faces) carrying a\nDirac-type operator `D = sum_e c(alpha_e) nabla_e + Gamma phi`: null\ncovector solderings `alpha_e` (Clifford coefficients `c(alpha)^2 = 0`),\ncovariant edge transports `nabla_e`, and a vertex \"turn\" term (the\nHiggs-shaped channel). A kernel-checked Weitzenboeck identity splits\n`4 D^#D` into four channels: aperture `Q_A` (kinetic, ties to det P),\nclosure `Q_C` (gauge/QCD-shaped: commutators of transports), turn `Q_T`\n(Yukawa-shaped), soldering-gradient `E` (gravity-shaped). Slogan:\nunification is decomposition. `#` is a Krein-space adjoint - the\ngeometry is indefinite by construction, and positivity holds (at best)\nonly on a constrained physical sector, Gupta-Bleuler style.\n\n**Protection.** Finite McKean-Singer index theorems (kernel-checked):\nan unbalanced chirality count forces exact massless modes immune to\nevery potential and transport.\n\n**Dynamics.** Kernel-checked this week: one decimation (Schur\ncomplement) step converts per-edge null structure into non-null\neffective terms with coefficient exactly the pairwise Clifford pairing\n(`(ab)^2 = k(ab)` for square-zero a, b with anticommutator k); the\neffective coupling vanishes identically for collinear pairs. So the\nSAME disagreement invariant that is mass kinematically becomes mass\ndynamically under coarse-graining.\n\n**QCD lane.** Kernel-checked at strong coupling on concrete finite\nlattices: Wilson-loop area law, slab reflection positivity, a\ntransfer/OS spectral gap, exponential clustering, character-expansion\nbounds. Also: the Wilson plaquette action is EXACTLY the squared\nHilbert-Schmidt norm of the closure defect `1 - holonomy` (one-line\nidentity, now pinned). This week's sharpest structural result (verified\nanalytically + by two independent numerical oracles, Lean transcription\nin progress): the nonabelian closure channel is an EXACT Krein square,\n`Q_C = L^# L`, with an explicit gauge-covariant current\n`L = c(alpha_1) (x) 1 + c(alpha_2) (x) (-K/2)`, `K = [nabla_1,nabla_2]`,\nfor ANY compact group - but Krein squares carry no positivity by\nthemselves, which relocates the whole positivity question (challenge\nC2).\n\n## 4. Status snapshot (end of 2026-07-07)\n\n- Kernel-checked: the section-2 theorem; the 4-channel Weitzenboeck\n  split; index-protection family; the decimation/mass-generation\n  witness with its collinear control; the strong-coupling QCD stack\n  above; exact finite Ginsparg-Wilson chirality structure whose grading\n  is edge-orientation reversal; a nonvacuous positive Gupta-Bleuler\n  quotient witness on a small model; color-fiber structure (particle\n  states as exterior-algebra \"strand\" monomials; hypercharge derived;\n  lepton/baryon number = strand-count grading).\n- MEMO grade: Standard-Model representation selection (five-strand\n  principle); three generations via triality-as-monodromy (multiplicity\n  menu {1,3} forced); equipartition trace identity behind the Koide\n  combination.\n- KILLED this week (pre-registered probes): see section 6.\n- DISCOVERED this week: (i) every cyclically symmetric celestial\n  decoration of a transport cycle pins an EXACT zero quasi-energy mode\n  (generic decorations sit near zero); geometric decorations self-lock\n  onto the abstract \"massless locus\"; (ii) the closure channel is\n  chromomagnetic (linear in field strength), NOT energy-shaped - see\n  pitfall P2.\n\n## 5. The hardest challenges (the ask)\n\n**C1 - a finite forest-counting injection (pure combinatorics; our\nsingle most-resisted target: five failed expert-prover attempts).**\nSetting: cluster/polymer expansion (Penrose-style tree partition\nscheme). We need: for a fixed rooted-forest target with given block\nsizes `m_j` summing appropriately, an explicit injection from\n{fiber element} x Perm(k) x prod_j Perm(m_j) into orderings of n\nlabeled objects (root-first, blocks in sigma-order, internally\nreordered), with fiber constancy - the missing bound is\n`|fiber| * k! * prod m_j! <= n!`. The intended proof is a left-inverse\n(parse the ordering back), with canonical-least-root and\nincreasing-children constraints as tie-breaks. GUIDANCE SOUGHT: a\nclean certificate/encoding argument, or a reference where exactly this\nfiber bound is proved (Penrose 1967 descendants; Fernandez-Procacci),\nor a reformulation that avoids the injection entirely.\n\n**C2 - constraint-compatible representatives in a Krein space (the\npositivity crux).** The closure channel is an exact Krein square\n`Q_C = L^# L` whose representation family is a GL-torsor\n{`L_A = c(alpha_1)(x)A + c(alpha_2)(x)(A^dag)^{-1}(-K/2)`}. On the FULL\nspace the form `[psi, Q_C psi]` is maximally indefinite (necessarily -\nnull Clifford coefficients are #-isotropic). Positivity can only hold\non a physical sector `V'/N` (Gauss-law kernel modulo null vectors), and\ndescends IF some torsor member preserves `V'` and its constraint\nsubspace. QUESTION: for lattice gauge Gauss constraints, is there a\nprincipled construction of a constraint-compatible representative\n(e.g. via gauge averaging, BRST-style doublets, or a positive-definite\ndeformation)? Any literature on \"operator squares in Krein spaces\nrestricted to Pontryagin-positive sectors\"? A no-go would also be\nvaluable: if the restricted form must stay indefinite, chiral symmetry\nbreaking wants it that way (the curvature term must pull eigenvalues\ndown), and we need the sharp statement of that trade-off.\n\n**C3 - multi-direction closure representation.** The exact square above\nis proved for TWO transport directions. With d >= 3 directions,\n`Q_C = -sum_{mu<nu} b_{mu nu} (x) [nabla_mu, nabla_nu]` and per-pair\ncurrents generate cross-pair symmetric contaminations. CONJECTURE\n(pre-registered): a compensated form `Q_C + (aperture-shaped\ncorrection) = L^# L`. GUIDANCE: is there a known algebraic identity\n(Fierz-like, or from Clifford bimodule theory) organizing\n`sum_pairs (bivector) (x) (curvature)` as a single square plus a\ncanonical symmetric remainder?\n\n**C4 - the right invariant for symmetry-forced zero modes.** Finding:\na length-V cycle of SU(2) frame transports built from V celestial\ndirections arranged symmetrically on ANY cone pins an exact unit\neigenvalue of the (unitary) transfer; random decorations give only\nnear-zero modes; in abstract (non-geometric) data the zero mode needs a\ncodim-1 locus relating amplitude and Berry-phase holonomy - which\ngeometric decorations satisfy AUTOMATICALLY. Discrete-time quantum-walk\ntopology (Kitagawa et al.; Asboth et al.: 0- and pi-quasienergy\ninvariants) is clearly adjacent but is about boundary states of walks,\nnot decoration-forced spectral pinning on closed loops. QUESTION: what\nis the correct invariant/theorem class here - equivariant index on the\ncircle? spectral flow with a symmetry constraint? Kramers-like pinning\nfrom an antiunitary in the cyclic group's presence?\n\n**C5 - is any honest mass-VALUE route still alive?** Our Koide-relation\nmechanism died by its own pre-registered probe: the dynamically\nmeasured soldering coefficient is kappa = 3/2 (predicting Q = 5/9),\nnot the kappa = 1 (Q = 2/3) the relation needs, and the reduction does\nnot even produce the uniform-diagonal ansatz. Any surviving route must\nALSO clear Sumino's bar (family symmetry protecting the relation\nagainst QED running). QUESTION: given a finite mechanism that produces\nmass RATIOS from cycle geometry (spectra like pi/6 {0,1,2} arise\nnaturally), what observable targets are honest at finite level -\nneutrino mass-squared-difference ratios? Koide-like relations for other\nsectors? Or should finite programs stop at structural statements?\n\n**C6 - what continuum statements are realistically provable?** Our\nrule: quotient-then-limit (algebraic quotients at finite level, limits\nonly through a benchmark ladder of refinement maps; the exact GW\nstructure and a Wilson-term identity are the anchors). QUESTION: for a\nprogram that will NOT attempt constructive Yang-Mills, what is the\nstrongest continuum-facing theorem class worth targeting - graph/\noperator convergence of Dirac spectra under refinement (Laplacian\nrenormalization-group style), Gromov-Hausdorff-type spectral\nconvergence, or categorical colimits of transfer data?\n\n**C7 - discrete torsion split of the gravity channel.** The\nsoldering-gradient channel E is NOT pure discrete torsion (our earlier\nconjecture died by probe). Pre-registered target: an exact split\n`2E = Contract(T) + Contract(S)` with T the antisymmetrized soldering\ndifference (torsion-shaped) and S a symmetric remainder\n(non-metricity-shaped) - a finite \"geometric trinity\" statement.\nGUIDANCE: pointers to teleparallel/symmetric-teleparallel\ndecompositions done at simplicial/discrete level (Regge-adjacent),\nand the right discrete definition of contorsion.\n\n## 6. Closed routes and pitfalls (do not spend time here)\n\n- P1. \"Q_C = sum over faces of (1 - holonomy)^dag (1 - holonomy)\"\n  (site-diagonal defect Gram) is DEAD structurally: the closure channel\n  is purely off-diagonal in the site grading; any site-local Gram is\n  exactly Frobenius-orthogonal to it. (The defect Gram is a fine object\n  - it is the Wilson ACTION - it is just not this operator.)\n- P2. Do not conflate the two closure objects: the defect GRAM is\n  |F|^2-shaped (energy); the Weitzenboeck closure CHANNEL is linear in\n  F (chromomagnetic, sigma.F). In mass-budget language the channel's\n  share is hyperfine-shaped (Delta-N, rho-pi splittings), not \"99\n  percent of the proton\".\n- P3. Koide via tetrahedral corner geometry: killed by measurement\n  (kappa = 3/2). The equipartition trace identity survives as algebra.\n- P4. \"Trace of E = discrete torsion\": killed by probe.\n- P5. Retardedness alone cannot delete fermion doublers (proved:\n  determinant-level obstruction); one-sided Ginsparg-Wilson inversion\n  is false in nonabelian settings (explicit 2x2 counterexample) -\n  palindromic/midpoint transfer ordering is the correct convention.\n- P6. Spectral-measure language is embargoed program-wide until the C2\n  positivity question resolves; finite eigenvalue-COUNT identities are\n  the sanctioned form (e.g. our finite Banks-Casher target\n  `m V Sigma_m = N_m`).\n- P7. i.e. is null in the quotient. -/\n151\ttheorem kreinForm_pos_definite_on_quotient (x : W) (hx : x ∈ LinearMap.ker Qop)\n152\t    (h0 : (kreinForm Jpos x x).re = 0) : x ∈ LinearMap.range Qop := by\n153\t  have h_expansion : (kreinForm Jpos x x).re = x.ofLp 2 * star x.ofLp 2 := by\n154\t    unfold Qop at *\n155\t    simp_all +decide [Matrix.toEuclideanLin]\n156\t    simp_all +decide [Qmat, Matrix.toLpLin]\n157\t    unfold kreinForm Jpos at h0\n158\t    simp_all +decide\n159\t    unfold GmatPos at h0\n160\t    simp_all +decide [Matrix.toEuclideanLin, Matrix.vecHead, [Qmat]\n168\t    exact hx\n169\t  refine' ⟨EuclideanSpace.single 1 (x.ofLp 0), _⟩\n170\t  rw [hx_form]\n171\t  ext i\n172\t  fin_cases i <;> norm_num [Qop, Qmat]\n173\t\n174\t/-! ### The nonvacuous positive-sector witness -/\n175\t\n176\t/-- Nonvacuous positive-sector witness, with inertia `(2,1)`.  `Q` is a nilpotent\n177\tKrein-self-adjoint charge for `Jpos`, so `finite_kugo_ojima` applies.  The\n178\tKugo-Ojima quotient `ker Q / range Q` is nonvacuous, the class of `e₂` is\n179\tnonzero, and the induced form is positive definite on representatives modulo\n180\t`range Q`. -/\n181\ttheorem nonvacuous_positive_sector :\n182\t    (Jpos ∘ₗ Jpos = LinearMap.id) ∧ (Qop ∘ₗ Qop = 0) ∧ (kreinAdjoint Jpos Qop = Qop) ∧\n183\t    (∀ x ∈ LinearMap.ker Qop, x ∉ LinearMap.range Qop →\n184\t      ∃ y ∈ LinearMap.ker Qop, kreinForm Jpos y x ≠ 0) ∧\n185\t    (e2 ∈ LinearMap.ker Qop ∧ e2 ∉ LinearMap.range Qop) ∧\n186\t    (∀ x ∈ LinearMap.ker Qop, 0 ≤ (kreinForm Jpos x x).re) ∧\n187\t    (∀ x ∈ LinearMap.ker Qop, (kreinForm Jpos x x).re = 0 → x ∈ LinearMap.range Qop) ∧\n188\t    0 < (kreinForm Jpos e2 e2).re := by\n189\t  refine ⟨Jpos_involutive, Qop_sq, Qop_kreinAdjoint_pos, ?_, ⟨e2_mem_ker, e2_not_mem_range⟩,\n190\t    kreinForm_pos_nonneg_on_ker, kreinForm_pos_definite_on_quotient, ?_⟩\n191\t  · exact (finite_kugo_ojima Jpos Jpos_involutive Qop Qop_sq Qop_kreinAdjoint_pos).2.2\n192\t  · rw [kreinForm_pos_e2]\n193\t    norm_num\n194\t\n195\t/-! ### The sharp no-go -/\n196\t\n197\t/-- Sharp no-go, with inertia `(1,2)`.
